宏观经济深度研究 - 2026 年 10 月 13 日 1

宏观经济深度研究 
宏观稳定的微观基石 
——2026 年诺贝尔经济学奖点评

“见微知著，睹始知终。”2026 年诺贝尔经济学奖花落罗伯特·霍尔与迈克尔·伍德
福德，以表彰两位学者为 “宏观经济稳定的微观基础 ”作出的开创性贡献。二人
的研究共同推动了宏观经济学从观察总量变化，走向理解总量背后的微观行为
机制。这在当前全球经济冲击来源日益复杂、周期性波动与结构性变化相互交
织的背景下，具有鲜明的现实意义。随着全球供应链重构、人口结构转变、地
缘政治风险以及技术变革等深层因素不断重塑经济运行模式，相似的总量数据
背后可能对应着截然不同的微观动因。因此，精准识别经济波动背后的微观行
为逻辑，并通过清晰、稳定且可信的政策信号引导家庭与企业形成良好的长期
预期，对提升宏观政策有效性愈发关键。这一理论也与当前中国宏观调控着力
扩大内需、稳定社会预期、增强政策取向一致性的导向高度契合。随着中国经
济体量持续扩大、金融市场不断深化以及市场主体日益多元，预期渠道在宏观
政策传导中的作用正日益凸显。从这一视角来看，稳预期既是稳定经济运行的
重要目标，也是强化宏观政策传导，进一步释放消费与投资潜力的重要机制。 
新晋诺奖获得者共同奠定了宏观稳定政策的微观基础。 2026 年诺贝尔经济学奖
授予罗伯特·霍尔（Robert E. Hall ）与迈克尔·伍德福德（Michael Woodford），以
表彰二人为 “宏观经济稳定的微观基础 ”作出的开创性贡献。霍尔现为斯坦福大
学经济学教授，曾于 2010 年担任美国经济学会主席，并长期担任美国国家经济
研究局（NBER）商业周期测定委员会主席。作为一位研究领域十分广泛的应用
宏观经济学家，霍尔长期关注消费、就业、通胀、技术、市场竞争以及宏观经
济政策。伍德福德现任哥伦比亚大学政治经济学教授，认知与行为经济学计划
主任，同时也是 NBER 行为宏观经济学工作组的创始联合主任。他的研究主要
集中于有限理性对经济分析的影响，在货币政策传导的微观基础、利率与通胀
决定、最优货币政策规则、中央银行沟通以及货币与财政政策互动等领域作出
了系统贡献。两人的共同贡献，在于推动宏观经济学从观察总量关系进一步走
向理解总量背后的微观行为机制，这促进了宏观稳定理论的范式转换，如今已
广泛应用于中央银行和宏观政策分析之中。如果说 2025 年诺贝尔经济学奖回答
了“经济增长从何而来”的长期命题，那么 2026 年的诺奖则将目光重新拉回经济
周期的另一面：当冲击无法避免，政策如何帮助经济恢复稳定？增长决定经济
能够走多远，稳定则决定经济如何穿越周期。霍尔与伍德福德从不同方向给出
了一个共同答案：宏观经济并不是独立于微观世界之外运行的庞然大物，消
费、就业、通胀与产出等总量指标，最终是无数家庭、劳动者和企业选择的集
合。霍尔将家庭消费从当期收入驱动的经验关系，重塑为基于未来预期的跨期
选择问题，揭示微观主体如何依据信息变化调整当前行为。伍德福德则将家庭
消费、企业定价、价格粘性与前瞻预期统一于现代货币政策框架，解释央行为
什么能够通过利率路径和预期管理稳定经济。从这一意义上看， 2026 年的诺奖
不仅是对两位宏观经济学家的肯定，也是对过去半个世纪宏观经济学演进方向
的一次总结：宏观结果由微观行为集聚而成，只有看见微观行为如何形成宏观
结果，宏观政策才能真正理解经济，并进一步稳定经济。 
从经验到预期，微观理解重塑消费与货币政策理论。 霍尔最具奠基意义的突破
来自消费理论。在传统凯恩斯函数中，家庭消费主要随着当期可支配收入变
化。而生命周期 —永久收入理论指出，家庭消费并不只取决于当期收入，还取
决于其对终身收入和财富的预期，并据此在不同阶段之间平滑消费。霍尔的核
心观点就在于，如果家庭已经利用所有已知信息决定当期消费，那么只有新的
信息才能改变其对未来收入的判断。但根据定义，新信息无法预测（否则它就

证券研究
不是新信息了）。那么在一定前提条件下，消费的变化同样不应该被已有信息系
统性预测，因为它依赖于前瞻性家庭、理性预期等模型条件。在这一点上，霍
尔颠覆了传统的研究方法，他认为研究人员不应寻找能够预测家庭消费的指
标，而是应该检验 “消费变化无法预测 ”这一假设。微观研究给予了这一假设较
强支持，霍尔利用家庭层面数据发现，大约八成家庭的消费行为符合前瞻性消
费理论，其余家庭则更多受到当期收入和流动性条件约束。这一区别后来成为
现代宏观模型刻画不同家庭行为的重要基础。如果说霍尔更多解释了家庭和劳
动力市场如何形成宏观波动，那么伍德福德则系统回答了稳定政策如何通过这
些微观主体发挥作用。其研究将家庭跨期消费、企业最优定价、价格粘性和理
性预期纳入统一框架，推动现代新凯恩斯货币政策理论走向系统化。这一理论
最核心的贡献之一，是将霍尔的研究成果，即消费取决于家庭对其未来收入的
预期纳入模型。此外，伍德福德进一步将利率纳入家庭跨期消费决策框架，解

释央行如何通过改变实际利率影响消费与总需求。当实际利率高于自然利率
时，当前消费的相对成本上升，家庭倾向于推迟消费、增加储蓄，总需求随之
受到抑制；反之，当实际利率低于自然利率时，家庭更倾向于将未来消费提前
至当期，从而扩张总需求。因此，中央银行影响经济活动的关键，并不一定是
改变货币数量，也可以通过调整政策利率相对于自然利率的位置，改变家庭跨
期配置消费的选择。此外，伍德福德通过企业定价行为的处理进一步完善了这
一框架。在传统凯恩斯主义中，价格粘性长期被视为需求变化能够影响实际产
出和就业的重要原因，但为什么价格具有粘性、企业又如何决定调整价格，并
没有坚实的微观基础。伍德福德从单个企业的最优化决策出发解释这一问题：
现实中的企业调整价格存在成本，因此不会随着每一次需求和生产成本变化立
即重新定价；但一旦企业获得调整价格的机会，其决定又必然具有前瞻性。如
果企业预期未来自身成本上升、竞争对手价格提高或整体通胀走高，那么短期
就有动力设定更高的价格。当前通胀不只是当前供需状况的结果，也与企业对

未来经济环境的判断密切相关。在这一点上，家庭消费和企业定价具有一个共
同特征，即当下的行为依赖其对未来的预期。
预期如何放大冲击，也将如何稳定增长。 这套建立在微观行为与前瞻预期基础
上的框架，进一步改变了宏观政策识别冲击和稳定经济的方式。由于价格调整
存在摩擦，需求变化不会立即完全体现为价格变化，而会同时影响实际产出与
通胀。实际产出与价格充分灵活条件下 “自然产出”之间的差距，即产出缺口，
由此成为判断经济冷热和通胀压力的重要依据。这一框架也解释了为什么相似
的通胀表现可能需要不同的政策反应。如果通胀源于需求过度扩张，提高利率
可以压低消费和投资，使经济重新向自然产出靠拢；但如果通胀来自能源价格
上涨、供应链中断等负向供给冲击，经济本身能够实现的自然产出已经下降，
此时过度收紧需求反而可能进一步加剧产出损失。稳定政策因此首先需要识别
冲击究竟来自需求还是供给，再判断家庭消费、企业定价和经济活动将如何变
化。微观基础的价值正在于此：它不仅让模型更加严谨，也使政策制定从观察
表面的宏观数据进一步走向理解数据背后的行为机制。更重要的是，前瞻性意

味央行即使没有立即改变利率，只要能够可信地改变市场对未来政策路径的判
断，就可能提前影响消费、定价与通胀。货币政策由此从单纯的 “利率行动”进
一步走向“预期管理”，政策透明度、连续性与可信度也成为政策传导机制的重
要组成部分。正是在这里，霍尔与伍德福德的研究真正汇合：预期成为连接政
策、微观行为与宏观结果的关键纽带。政策影响预期，预期改变行为，行为塑
造经济，而新的经济结果又反过来影响政策。理解微观，是理解宏观的起点；
理解预期，则是理解现代宏观稳定政策如何真正发挥作用的关键。
对全球与中国的启示。 当前，全球经济正在进入一个宏观冲击来源更加多元的
时代，政策制定者除了要面对传统周期的扩张与收缩，还需要面对供应链、能
源、人口结构、地缘事件和人工智能等结构性冲击。在这一环境中，仅凭通
胀、就业和 GDP 等最终指标判断政策方向越来越困难，相同的数据表现可能由
完全不同的微观机制产生。 对于主要经济体而言， 在不确定性上升的环境中，

### Page 3

宏观经济深度研究 - 2026 年 10 月 13 日 3 
证券研究 
稳定政策预期有助于降低家庭和企业的观望情绪，也能够减少金融市场对于政
策路径的过度波动。 一方面，政策制定需要更加准确地区分冲击来源 。如果通
胀来自需求过热，提高利率能够通过抑制当前消费和投资缓解价格压力；但如
果通胀更多源于能源、供应链等供给冲击，过度压低需求反而可能带来更大的
产出损失。因此，宏观政策不能仅依据通胀率、失业率等最终结果机械调整，
而需要进一步判断家庭为何改变消费、企业为何调整价格，以及这些行为是短
期冲击还是长期预期变化所致。政策的有效性不只取决于力度，更取决于能否
准确作用于经济波动背后的微观机制。 另一方面，宏观政策需要更加重视可信
度和一致性。 霍尔和伍德福德的研究表明，预期管理本身正在成为宏观政策的
重要组成部分。家庭并不会简单依据当前收入决定消费，而会判断收入变化是
否具有持久性；企业也会根据未来利率、成本和价格水平决定短期的投资与定
价。政策连续性、透明度与可信度由此不只是沟通问题，更是决定政策传导效
率的重要宏观资源。清晰且可信的中长期政策路径，可以在政策落地之前就影
响消费、投资和价格。 对于中国而言， 随着经济规模扩大、金融市场深化和市
场主体日益多元，预期渠道正在成为宏观政策传导的重要组成部分。霍尔与伍
德福德的研究与当前宏观政策更加注重扩大内需、稳定预期和增强政策协同的
方向具有较强的现实契合度。2026 年《政府工作报告》强调增强政策的“前瞻性
针对性协同性”，着力“稳就业、稳企业、稳市场、稳预期 ”，同时提出增强宏观
政策取向一致性和有效性，并健全预期管理机制、提振社会信心。货币政策层
面，则进一步强调畅通货币政策传导机制，引导金融机构加力支持扩大内需等
重点领域。这一意义上看，随着预期管理在宏观政策中的重要性不断提升，稳
定就业和收入预期、增强政策协同并畅通传导机制，将有助于更好地将政策支
持转化为居民消费和企业投资动力，进一步释放内需潜力。

重要披露 
工银国际的股票评级 
超越基准 ：预期未来 12 个月内股票总回报（包括股息）超越相应市场基准指数 20%以上 
中性 ：预期未来 12 个月内股票总回报（包括股息）对比相应市场基准指数在-20%至 20%以内 
落后基准 ：预期未来 12 个月内股票总回报（包括股息）落后相应市场基准指数 20%以上 
暂无评级 ：分析师目前对于预期未来 12 个月内股票总回报（包括股息）对比相应市场基准指数的变化幅度无法给出确信的判断

Update
Hiwin Technologies Corp.  | Asia Pacific
September Sales Flat MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Hiwin's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$2,720mn (flat MoM/+30% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$8,110mn (+8% QoQ/+35% YoY), largely in line
with our estimate at NT$7,996mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY) and the  consensus
of NT$7,964mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY).
Our view:
• On top of the sequential growth on the top line, we believe that Hiwin

should also further expand its gross margin on a QoQ basis in 3Q26, owing
to the higher utilization and the tailwinds of price hikes.
• We think the momentum should remain favorable in the near term.
• We stay constructive on the stock, with current valuation at 29x 2027e P/E
vs. a peak cycle level of 35-40x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.

n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

Document 171: Anthropic估值预期承压：收入增速放缓，部分投资者转向1.5万亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic估值预期承压：收入增速放缓，部分投资者转向1.5万亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

据媒体报道， Anthropic Q3 营收环⽐增⻓ 45% ，远不及 Q2 逾 140% 的增速，部分投资者认
为，承销商应下调 IPO 估值预期，估值可能低于 1.5 万亿美元，⽽此前部分投⾏私下讨论的
⽔平约为 2 万亿美元。 Jefferies 分析师表⽰，华尔街可能预期 Anthropic 的年化收⼊⾄少达
到 800 亿美元，并希望年底前超过 1050 亿美元。
摘要：
Anthropic 第三季度收⼊继续⾼速增⻓，但增速明显放缓，令部分 IPO 潜在投资者开始重新审视
这家 AI 公司的估值预期。在 AI 交易承压、融资成本上升的背景下，市场对⾼增⻓能否⽀撑⾼估值
的疑虑正在加深。
据《 The Information 》报道， Anthropic 在周三举⾏的旧⾦⼭投资者会议上披露，第三季度收⼊
达到 168 亿美元，环⽐增⻓约 45% ，低于第⼆季度超过 140% 的增幅。
部分潜在投资者认为，承销商应下调 IPO 估值预期，估值可能低于 1.5 万亿美元，⽽此前部分投

⾏私下讨论的⽔平约为 2 万亿美元。
⾏业竞争加剧， OpenAI 正在加强企业级 AI 产品，并通过更低价格竞争； Google 、 Meta 等公司的
新模型，以及开源模型，也在给 Anthropic 带来压⼒。
Anthropic 增⻓放缓为已经⾯临通胀和利率上⾏压⼒的 AI 交易再添不确定性。市场不仅关注 AI 公
司的收⼊规模，也开始审视增⻓预期能否兑现，以及巨额芯⽚和基础设施投⼊能否获得相应回
报。
收⼊增速明显放缓，增⻓预期⾯临考验
Anthropic 第三季度收⼊从第⼆季度的 116 亿美元增⾄ 168 亿美元，较第⼀季度的 47 亿美元继续⼤
幅增⻓。但推动公司此前收⼊快速攀升的动⼒正在发⽣变化。
第⼆季度收⼊环⽐增⻓超过 140% ，主要受 Claude Code 需求激增推动；第三季度环⽐增速则降
⾄约 45% 。尽管收⼊仍在快速扩张，但增速降档可能影响投资者对公司未来增⻓路径的判断。
收⼊结构也存在变化。 Anthropic 估计，模型蒸馏相关收⼊约占总收⼊的 5% ⾄ 7% ，公司预计这

部分收⼊将在当前季度下降，并继续拖累收⼊表现。模型蒸馏是指其他公司利⽤现有 AI 模型的输
出训练⾃⼰的模型。
Anthropic 已连续两个季度实现调整后盈利，三季度现⾦流也转为正值。公司披露的年内调整后
营业利润率为 11% ，但这⼀指标不包括股票薪酬等重⼤成本。
部分投资者重新评估 IPO 估值
收⼊增速放缓正对 Anthropic 的 IPO 估值预期构成压⼒。
部分潜在投资者对最新财务数据感到失望，认为承销商需要降低估值预期，部分投资者讨论的
估值⽔平可能低于 1.5 万亿美元，低于此前部分投⾏私下提出的约 2 万亿美元。
这⼀变化反映的是投资者对估值的重新讨论，并不意味着 Anthropic 已经正式下调 IPO 定价。对
于潜在投资者⽽⾔，关键问题在于，公司能否持续实现⾜够快的收⼊增⻓，以⽀撑此前的⾼估
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

值预期。
Jefferies 分析师 Jeffrey Favuzza 表⽰，华尔街可能预期 Anthropic 的年化收⼊运⾏率⾄少达到
800 亿美元，并希望年底前超过 1050 亿美元。市场对增⻓的要求已经很⾼，因此，即使收⼊继续
增⻓，若未达到预期，也可能引发估值压⼒。
巨额基础设施投⼊加剧回报压⼒
收 ⼊ 增 ⻓ 与 盈 利改 善 之 外 ， Anthropic 还 需 要 应 对 庞 ⼤ 的 基 础 设施 投 ⼊ 。 媒 体 此 前 报 道 ，
Anthropic 计划未来⼗年在芯⽚和基础设施等⽅⾯投⼊超过 5000 亿美元。
如此规模的投⼊，使收⼊增⻓能否持续转化为现⾦流和利润成为估值讨论的重要部分。在融资
成本上升的环境下，投资者也更关注 AI 企业能否以合理的成本扩⼤业务，⽽⾮仅仅追求收⼊规

模。
Anthropic 财务披露引发市场关注之际， AI 相关资产已经⾯临更⼴泛的压⼒。投资者对 AI 云服务
商 CoreWeave 的财务状况存在担忧，韩国存储芯⽚股也出现⼤幅下跌。与此同时，市场还在消
化通胀数据偏热及利率可能维持⾼位的⻛险。
这些因素并不意味着 AI 需求正在全⾯减弱，但它们正在改变市场评估 AI 企业的⽅式。对于需要持
续投⼊⼤量资⾦的公司⽽⾔，融资成本、收⼊增⻓和投资回报之间的关系正受到更严格的审
视。

Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

As of year-end 2025, Bloom's product backlog grew about 140% YoY to roughly
US$6bn. Amid the surging demand, Bloom is improving backlog conversion and
stepping up capacity expansion to capture the strong growth opportunity. In 2Q26,
Bloom raised its full-year revenue guidance to US$3.9-4.2bn, up from US$3.4-3.8bn,
and plans to double annual capacity from about 1GW in 2025, split across its California

and Delaware sites, to 2GW by the end of 2026; and will further expand the capacity
to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our channel checks.

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

High-speed products a new driver
In 3Q24, ACES passed product qualification with ODMs for the high-speed
connectors used on Nvidia GB200 networking cards (NICs) and accelerator cards,
marking its move from traditional PC peripherals into the server space and Nvidia
supply chain. In 2025, server revenue accounted for 10% of ACES’s total revenue.
Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s server business to grow 107% in 2026, thanks to

its new product offerings, higher content per rack of high-speed products, and
strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.
Figure 13 Figure 14
ACES high-speed connector content per rack increase ACES provides MCIO and a wide range of high-speed connectors

Source: SinoPac Source: ACES
Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Figure 29 Figure 30
ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

Under the twin pressures of softening end-consumer demand and continued

supply-chain cost increases, we expect ACES’s full-year NB revenue to decrease
12% in 2026 and return to a growth trajectory in 2027. However, we remain
cautiously optimistic about 2027 with the launch of Windows 12, combined with
easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Diversifying into new growth opportunities
We found that ACES is diversifying its products’ end-application by developing new

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

ACES Enters from Gen6
with Vertical Integration
and flexible lead time

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

### Page 2

Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

從台灣科技及半導體  3Q26 營收找全球科技及半導體產業景氣趨勢
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
OCT 12 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
Executive summary

台灣半導體暨科技產業公布 9 ⽉份及 3Q26 營收，我們以公布的數據看產業趨勢
變化及公司與彭博社分析師預期進⾏分析⽐較，投資⼈須審慎評估。總⽽⾔
之， 3Q26 營收明顯超過彭博社分析師預期的公司⼤幅多於低於預期的公司達 6
倍多，⽽超過 53% 年增（⽤晶圓代⼯的 9 ⽉份營收年增做標竿）的有 6 個次產
業。除此以外，我們還提供了 3Q26 營收年增明顯改善及惡化的前 20 名公司整
理及排序，及 3Q26 營收明顯季增 / 季減的前 20 名公司整理及排序。總結⽽
⾔，伺服器產業鏈（對標公司有 Nvidia, AMD, Intel, ⾕歌 TPU ， Dell,
SuperMicro) ，存儲記憶體（對標公司有三星 , SK Hynix, SanDisk,
Western Digital) ， CCL/PCB ，半導體建廠及測試產業 3Q26 營收（對標公司
有  Teradyne, Advantest, Form Factor ）明顯⾼於分析師預期， 3Q26 營收
年增改善明顯的有存儲記憶體，光纖通訊（對標公司有 Lumentum,

Fabrinet, Coherent ）， CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備產業。 3Q26 營收季增
明顯改善的產業有 CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備產業，設計服務（對標公司有
Broadcom, Marvell) ，存儲記憶體，光纖通訊，及伺服器產業鏈。

September analysis on sectors
9 ⽉  yoy > 8 ⽉  yoy 10 個百分點以上： 9 ⽉份營收年增率⽐ 8 ⽉明顯加
速（ >=10%) 的僅有 2 個次產業，其中包括設計服務的  173% yoy （由
世芯⽉增 14% ，年增 496% 出貨 Trainium 3 給亞⾺遜所帶動）及
CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備的  48% yoy ( 主要是由台積電產業鏈的弘
塑，均華，志聖，印能所帶動）；
9 ⽉  yoy < 8 ⽉  yoy 10 個百分點以上：最近我們觀察到部分 AI 伺服器
及 PC 產業鏈組裝下游如廣達及緯創，滑軌⿓頭川湖，電源管理晶⽚茂達
及⼒智，散熱系統的雙鴻，及均熱⽚的健策，半導體建廠⼯程的漢唐，亞

短期電⼒短缺及短料缺貨卡⻑料造成的庫存調整，這間接造成 9 ⽉份營收

鏈；
9 ⽉  yoy > 53% 的產業：我們以台積電所帶動的晶圓代⼯ 9 ⽉份營收
53% 做為指標 , 9 ⽉份營收年增率超過 53% 的產業 6 個次產業，其中包括
AI 伺服器 /PC/ 遊戲機組裝， CCL/PCB 產業，半導體設計服務，光纖通

9 ⽉  yoy < 20% 的產業： 8 ⽉份營收年增率低於 20% 增⻑的產業有 4
個，其中有 LCD ⾯板， 8”/12” 晶圓材料， CMOS 感測及觸控，及 PC 電⼒
功率產業，主要應該還是因為存儲記憶體漲價所造成的 PC 產業及⼿機產
業需求不振；

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (October 20)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week . Industrial 
production is expected to edge up from 
5.2% yoy in August to 5.3% in September, 
supported by firm external demand. 
Exports stayed strong, rising 36.3% yoy 
in September, driven by the regional AI -
electronics upcycle.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.3% yoy  in September. 
Household sentiment is weak amid 
uncertain job prospects, slower income 
growth and elevated precautionary 
savings, while subdued property prices 
continue to weigh on household wealth 
and consumption. Investment also stays 
weak, with fixed as set investment 
expected to contract further by 7.5% yoy 
ytd in September. Corporate investment 
is cautious amid the anti -involution 
campaign and geopolitical tensions, 
while the property sector continues to be 
a major drag.

Policymakers are likely to maintain a 
measured policy stance, relying on 
targeted fiscal support to sustain growth 
rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia and Bangko Sentral ng 
Pilipinas (Oct 21 and 22).

Bank Indonesia is expected to extend its 
pause, as they lean on non-rate measures, 
including intervention and hedging 
facilities to help the currency.  On Friday, 
MSCI signaled a  potential shift in 
Indonesia’s status to a standalone 
status, rather than a reclassification to 
frontier market , after consultations are 
complete in mid -2027. Any ensuing risk -
off move in the currency , would likely 
prompt greater central bank vigilance 
and intervention.

Our base case for the benchmark rate to 
be held unchanged this quarter , backed 
by a lower conviction around back -to-
back US rate hikes in Oct and relative 
rupiah stability. Separately, the 
government announced additional 
support for low-income households in 4Q 
by way of in -kind assistance and wage 
subsidies. This year’s fiscal deficit i s 
likely to close in at -2.85% of GDP, close 
to our forecast at -2.9%, but few 
uncertainties facing the 2027 math (note).

We expect a 25bp hike from the BSP to 
5.25% in October , after three rate 
increases earlier in the year. Rising by the 
highest pace in Asia, Philippines inflation 
surged to 7.2% y/y in September from 6.1% 
in August, matching this year's peak and 
approaching a three -year high.  Inflation 
risks remain tilted to the upside, driven by 
further public transport fare adjustments, 
elevated oil prices, recent wage hikes 
and peso depreciation. Fiscal space is, 
meanwhile, limited to support 
households, via subsidies and/ instate 
price caps. Governor Remolona also 
signalled that the BSP remains ready to 
act on rates to contain inflation. With 
inflationary pressures likely to be 
persistent, the likelihood of further hikes 
in 4Q26 rises.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Bank of Korea (October 22)

The Bank of Korea is expected to hold its 
policy rate at 3.00%, following back -to-
back rate hikes in July and August. AI -
driven growth momentum remains strong, 
as reflected in September export growth 
of around 80% YoY. However, the 
urgency for further tightening has eased, 
with CPI inflation stabilising at 2.9% YoY 
in September. The recent KOSPI 
correction and continued KRW strength 
also reduce the need for another near -
term hike. We maintain our call for a final 
25bp rate hike in November, bringing the 
policy rate to 3.25% by year-end.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Japan

September trade and CPI data are due 
shortly. Export growth is expected to 
remain solid at 24% YoY, supported by 
continued strength in AI -related 
semiconductor exports. CPI inflation is 
projected to rise notably to 2.6% YoY, 
driven by the phase -out of publ ic utility 
subsidies, higher global energy prices, 
and persistent inflationary pressures 
from a weak yen. The BOJ is likely to 
increasingly acknowledge that its 2% 
inflation target is within reach. However, 
it is expected to keep rates unchanged 
at 1.25% at its October 29–30 meeting to 
assess the impact of the latest rate hike 
in September.

Taiwan

September export orders and industrial 
production data are due shortly. Export 
orders are expected to sustain 60% YoY 
growth, while industrial production is 
projected to expand by 20% YoY. The 
global AI boom faces growing headwinds 
from hyperscalers’ cash flow pressures, 
central bank rate hikes, and higher bond 
yields. However, our high -frequency 
indicators across AI demand (token 
expenditure), investment (hyperscaler  
capex and data centre construction 
spending), and supply (semiconductor 
sales and exports) show no clear signs of 
a significant slowdown so far.

Malaysia

Malaysia’s external demand likely 
remained strong in September 2026, 
while inflation edged up slightly. We 
expect export growth to sustain its 
robust expansion at 45% yoy , with 
momentum anchored by strong demand 
for electrical and electronics goods. Oil 
& gas shipments likely remained buoyant. 
However, high base effects prevented a 
faster pace of expansion.

We expect headline inflation to rise 
modestly to 2.1% yoy in September 2026. 
The renewed increase in global oil prices 
likely contributed to higher increases in 
domestic energy costs, although fuel 
subsidies continued to limit the cost 
pass-through and keep underlying 
inflationary pressures contained. Food 
inflation likely also firmed, with adverse 
weather conditions associated with El 
Nino posing upside risks.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s core and 
headline inflation to edge up slightly to 
2.3% yoy and 2.4% yoy , respectively, in 
September 2026, following modest 
increases to 2.2% yoy and 2.3% yoy in 
August. Inflationary pressures likely 
reflected firm private transport price 
increases amid higher petrol prices 
despite slower car price growth , the 
ongoing pass -through of higher global 
energy prices to fuel -exposed services 
items, positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas prices, firmer 
food inflation, and a slight increase in 
still-muted accommodation inflation. 
Firm, albeit not acc elerating, inflation in 
September 2026, which may be close to 
its peak in the Monetary Authority of 
Singapore’s assessment, likely validated 
its decision to maintain SGD NEER policy 
settings at the October 2026 review, 
following two proactive tightening 
moves in April and July.

Hong Kong

CPI is projected to edge up from 1.7% yoy 
in August to 1.9% in September, 
supported by improving consumption 
sentiment and higher global energy 
prices. Mainland visitor arrivals 
increased by 29.0% yoy in September on

during the month has pushed up global 
energy prices, adding to upward 
pressure on utility and transport costs.

US manufacturing is gaining momentum, especially in terms of technology

Manufacturing is gaining momentum
Today has seen the release of another decent US industrial

production report. September output rose 0.7%MoM/2.2%YoY plus
there was a 0.1ppt upward revision to August's growth rate
(consensus was looking for a 0.4% gain overall). Manufacturing did
OK, rising 0.3% month-on-month, especially when we consider that
auto volumes fell 3%, while utilities surged 3.5% MoM as output is
ramped up to meet demand from data centres and expanding
manufacturers. Mining was up only 0.2% MoM.

manufacturing in the US. But so does US economic growth
continuing to outperform other key markets. Between 2023 and

2026, the US economy has grown an average of 2.5% year-on-year
in volume terms versus 0.9% in Europe.

Standard Chartered Bank

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained  
S&P500 volatility index (VIX); US bond volatility index (MOVE)*   10-year government bond yield spreads over German bonds

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *measures US government bond volatility

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro  area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro  
area growth and labour  market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near -term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads  are likely to  raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea , while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long -end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe s ystemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk  as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that  a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet , though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI -driven investment . The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets  remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny , with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions trigger ing fund gating  at some  private 
credit managers since Q 1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential . That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class , 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see m ajor US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on  
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth  of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI -related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile.  Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well -positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing , with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues  have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  USD/CHF has rebounded sharply over the past two 
months. What are the key factors behind the move, and do you 
see the currency pair continuing to move higher? 
Our view:  USD/CHF tested 0.84 amid strong momentum from 
the USD, but we see the pair remain ing supported near -term 
before gradually moving lower towards 0.82 in three months. 
Rationale: The Fed’s relatively high policy rate continues to support 
USD carry demand , while the Swiss National Bank maintains its 
policy rate at 0%. In the near term, however, the Swiss franc (CHF) 
could benefit from increased safe-haven demand amid mounting 
fiscal concerns in France  and political uncertainty in Spain , 
particularly if European risk premia continue to rise. Beyond these 
cyclical factors, Switzerland’s substantial net foreign asset position, 
persistent current account surpluses and low public debt burden 
continue to underpin the CHF’s structural strength . Over the 
medium term, we expect the USD’s rate advantage to gradually 
diminish as the Fed approaches the end of its tightening cycle, while 
the CHF’s defensive characteristics should remain supportive.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Do you see further upside for USD/JPY after recent dovish 
comments from BoJ officials and latest bond auctions? 
Our view: We see some near-term upside risk to USD/JPY from 
elevated US  yields, but retain our medium -term constructive 
JPY view. USD/JPY remains below 159, suggesting markets are 
mindful of intervention risk as the pair approaches 160. We 
continue to expect further BoJ normalisation. 
Rationale: Recent BoJ communication appears less dovish than the 
market narrative had suggested. BoJ Governor Ueda continues to 
emphasise the need to anchor underlying inflation around 2%  
and gradually reduce monetary accommodation, supporting our view 
that the tightening cycle is not yet over. An October pause would 
reflect a more measured pace of policy normalisation  rather than a 
change in the BoJ’s policy direction. 
Recent Japanese government bond (JGB) auctions have shown 
resilient demand despite higher yields.  At the latest 10 -year JGB 
auction, the bid -to-cover ratio improved  to 3.76x from 3.29x 
previously, while the auction tail (spread between highest 
accepted yield and issued yield) narrowed to 0.2bps from 1.6bps. 
We see this as indicative of an orderly, functioning market rather than 
a dovish BoJ signal . Intervention concerns around  the 160 level 
should help limit near-term USD/JPY upside.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

are likely to be offset by CHF safe-haven demand

### Page 1

AI 算⼒租赁⾏业正经历估值重构。 CoreWeave 单⽇暴跌 15.5% ，五年期 CDS 利差飙⾄ 1117
个基点，隐含违约概率⾼达 63% ； Gartner 警告， 2030 年前逾⼋成新兴云⼚商将⽆法独⽴
存续，⾏业⾯临减值⻛险资产超 3.5 万亿美元。超⽀与回报之间 4.9 万亿美元的剪⼑差，正
将这场 AI 盛宴推向信⽤危机边缘。
摘要：
AI 算⼒租赁⾏业正⾯临⼀场严峻的估值重构。信⽤市场对 CoreWeave 违约⻛险的定价持续飙
升，知名研究机构 Gartner 警告，预测逾⼋成新兴云计算服务商（ neocloud ）将在 2030 年前⽆法
独⽴存续。
CoreWeave 周三收跌 15.5% ，报 65.78 美元，盘中跌幅⼀度达 17% ，创上市以来最⼤单⽇跌幅纪
录。公司五年期信⽤违约互换（ CDS ）利差扩⼤ 135 个基点⾄历史新⾼ 1117 个基点，隐含五年内
违约概率约为 63% 。 CoreWeave ⾸席执⾏官 Michael Intrator 在⼀份内部备忘录中将市场反应归

咎于 " 误判 " ，称公司对⾃⾝商业模式充满信⼼ —— 然⽽备忘录发出后， CDS 利差反⽽继续⾛阔⾄
上述纪录⽔平。
Gartner 的报告为本已动荡的市场再添变量。该机构预计，到 2030 年 AI 基础设施⾏业⾯临减值⻛
险的资产规模将超过 3.5 万亿美元，并预测⾏业整体要到 2032 年底才有望达到 12% 的最低回报率
⻔槛。
Gartner 警告：⼋成新兴云⼚商将被淘汰
Gartner 在周三发布的报告中指出，到 2030 年，成本压⼒与缺乏差异化商业模式将迫使超过 80%
的 neocloud 提供商停⽌独⽴运营 ——这意味着每五家租⽤英伟达 GPU 的公司中，有四家将被并
购或直接退出市场。
Gartner 预计， AI 服务商仅 2026 年⼀年在 AI 基础设施上的⽀出就将接近 1 万亿美元， 2027 年⾄
2030 年间还将再投⼊约 6 万亿美元。然⽽，该⾏业预期能够获得的收⼊远低于⽀撑这⼀规模投资
所需的⽔平，两者之间存在约 4.9 万亿美元的缺⼝。
Gartner 同时预测，由于⼤多数 AI 原⽣公司采⽤轻资产模式， " 不⾜⼀半的 AI 收⼊将流向拥有重⼤

AI 基础设施的提供商 " 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

具体到 neocloud 所在的 AI 优化基础设施即服务（ IaaS ）赛道，其市场规模预计从 2027 年的 660
亿美元增⻓⾄ 2030 年的 1740 亿美元 ——Gartner 认为，在现有芯⽚被更新⼀代替代之前，这⼀
体量对于⼤多数 AI 算⼒提供商⽽⾔仍 " 不⾜以收回投资 " 。
值得注意的是， Gartner 今年 6 ⽉曾预测 neocloud 将在 2030 年的 2670 亿美元 AI 云市场中占据 20%
的份额。两份预测并不⽭盾：市场蛋糕变⼤了，但能分到蛋糕的玩家会⼤幅减少。
超⽀与回报的剪⼑差：⾏业结构性困局
CoreWeave 的财务状况清晰呈现了整个⾏业的结构性⽭盾。
截⾄ 6 ⽉末，公司总债务约为 520 亿美元，较⼀年前的约 150 亿美元⼤幅攀升。第⼆季度利息⽀出
从 2.67 亿美元跳升⾄ 6.4 亿美元，约占当季 25.8 亿美元营收的四分之⼀，同期净亏损达 6.26 亿美
元。
D.A. Davidson 分析师 Gil Luria 将问题浓缩为⼀句话：CoreWeave 的借款成本约为 9% ，⽽其投

资回报率仅约为 1% 。他警告称，⼀旦 AI ⽀出热潮降温， " 股权价值将归零 " 。
⾼盛估算得出的结论类似：六⼤美国科技⽀出商 2026 ⾄ 2027 年的算⼒投资，仅 2028 年⾄ 2030
年间便需产⽣约 1.4 万亿美元的收⼊⽅能收回成本，折合每吉⽡每年约 116 亿美元，⾏业所承诺
的收⼊规模，是当前终端需求所能实际消化的数倍。
信⽤市场的警报：从 CoreWeave 蔓延⾄整个⾏业
信⽤市场拉响的警报早于股票市场。
CoreWeave 的 12.5 亿美元 2032 年到期⽆担保债券收益率周⼆触及 15% ，随后在周三进⼀步⾛
阔， CDS 利差达到 1117 个基点的历史⾼位。
这场压⼒已蔓延⾄更⼴泛的 AI 融资⽣态。据⾼盛跨资产部⻔的报告，单个交易⽇内宏观信⽤利差
出现数⽉来最⼤扩张：甲⻣⽂ CDS 利差⾛阔 31 个基点，超⼤规模云⼚商现⾦债券利差⾛阔 4 ⾄ 5
个基点，数据中⼼债券利差⾛阔 7 个基点，⾼收益 AI 数据中⼼债券的表现与 CoreWeave ⾼度联

动。
底线：整合或许已是最乐观的情境

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

卖⽅机构或将 Gartner 的 80% 淘汰预测解读为⾏业整合的积极信号 —— 强者吸收弱者，幸存者以
折价获取 GPU ， AI 建设⼤潮继续推进。
这是⼀条可能的路径。然⽽，在 CDS 市场已将这⼀⾏业旗舰企业五年内违约概率定价逾六成的
背景下，更⼤概率的路径是：整合以资产减值、困境交换和重组顾问登⻔的形式到来， Gartner

机会 " ，但同时将 "AI 相关信贷事件 " 与 " 利差扩⼤ " 列为当前主要⻛险，且其中⼀项已在当⽇成
真。 AI ⾏业的融资逻辑正在接受市场最严格的压⼒测试。

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《聯準會升息並未牽動新興國家央行政策轉緊》
聯準會如市場預期在 9月FOMC會議上升息一碼至
3.75%~4%，美國10年期公債維持在 5%上下高位
震盪，不過資金並未逃離新興債市 ，截至9/16，資
金連續7週淨流入新興債市，其中以新興當地債淨流
入量為最。在資金支持下，即使7月以來美債殖利率
自4.37%一路攀升，新興當地債同期間僅下跌 0.4%，
新興主要貨幣主權債雖然跌幅達到2%，但利差仍維
持收斂走勢，也相對美債及成熟投資級債抗跌。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26殖利率目標價 投資展望

新興主要貨幣主權債指數 6.70% 較高的殖利率及穩健基本面將使其在利率波動時更為穩健
新興主要貨幣企業債指數 6.64% A~BB級企業相對穩健
新興投資級債指數 5.55% 亞洲企業債供給有限支撐買氣，中東良好債信吸引力仍佳
新興非投資級債指數 7.50% BB級具升等潛力，拉美非投資級債可望因油價上漲而受惠
新興當地貨幣債指數 6.80% 風險溢酬已反應貨幣政策較差情境，貨幣政策支持殖利率下行
人民幣債指數 2.20% 景氣不均衡復甦，寬鬆貨幣政策為主要支撐力
亞洲當地貨幣債指數 4.60% 亞洲國家貨幣政策觀望，短線維持中性看法

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 除了美國以外 ，主要成熟國家央行都採取了升息的
舉措：歐洲與日本央行也都在今年進行升息 ，英國
央行9月雖按兵不動，但態度也偏向鷹派，部分原因
來自全球通膨預期變化 ，這也和中東地緣紛擾不斷
有極大關係。全球新興國家除了中國和土耳其外，7
月通膨多明顯放緩 ，主要是能源價格下跌抵銷了大
部分3~5月通膨飆升的影響；然而趨動通膨放緩的
因素正在減弱 ，AI相關需求推升產品及大宗商品價
格，食品成本也有走升的現象 ，抵消服務業通膨的
降溫，加上近期中東局勢的升溫推動布蘭特原油來
到100美元以上 ，延續時間若拉長 ，能源價格恐將
反轉成為通膨上行壓力來源 ，尤其新興國家消費者
物價指數組成當中 ，能源相關占比相對成熟國家高 ，

長時間高油價將使部分新興市場通膨高於目標水平 ，
或限制央行政策放鬆的空間。
在美國利率上升的背景下 ，新興國家央行即便在國
內經濟有所放緩下 ，推動寬鬆貨幣政策的態度也將
更為謹慎，但這不代表新興國家將因此轉向升息 ，
例如與聯準會同一天召開利率會議的巴西央行便如
市場預期降息一碼至13.75%，主要是國內經濟放緩，
以及通膨數據和潛在通膨指標已跌破央行區間上限 ，
雖只是個案 ，但也顯示新興國家貨幣政策更趨向於
反應自身經濟環境 。在中東地緣政治仍可控的情境
下，除了巴西以外 ，土耳其、匈牙利、以色列等新
興國家目前預期仍朝降息方向發展 ，惟降息空間可
能收斂，其他新興國家則預估維持目前的利率政策
不變，雖然有部分亞洲國家在 AI需求帶動下擁有強
勁的經濟成長動能而給予升息的條件 ，但AI暢旺的
需求也使其經濟體更具對抗利率上升的韌性。

• 全球新興國家除了中國和土
